- Why: `"The migration changes the managed grammar from legacy planning to the v2 closure containing investigation.json, execution.json, and plan-review.json. This suite tests the stable filesystem and OpenSpec observation boundary that must accept those artifacts together before task or planning transitions use them."`
- Protected invariant: `"A managed v2 change is usable only when schema selection, canonical artifact membership, regular-file modes, exact snapshot digests, task and guard agreement, investigation applicability, execution policy, PlanReview, and adapter status agree in one observation. Partial, unsafe, contradictory, or concurrently mutated trees fail closed."`
- Reviewer question: `"The suite combines exact engine inspection with a fake OpenSpec status executable; which v2 readiness guarantees are directly proved and which real CLI behavior remains assumed?"`
- Answer: `"It directly proves stable mode-aware snapshot binding; refusal of missing applicability, wrong or duplicate metadata, unexpected or empty artifacts, executable or symlink entries, incomplete status, unsafe diagnostics, and mutation during status evaluation. It invokes production parsing for the three v2 artifacts and their relationships, while many adapter outcomes are fixture-produced. It does not claim a real OpenSpec installation or provider independently accepted the same tree."`

- Why: `"Every v2 planning transition relies on the OpenSpec adapter to discover the reviewed graph in which investigation.json, execution.json, and plan-review.json become complete only at their declared dependency points. This suite tests the adapter boundary rather than the semantic content of a model review."`
- Protected invariant: `"The adapter accepts only the exact reviewed OpenSpec installation and a closed internally consistent payload rooted in the expected repository. Schema source, artifact graph, output paths, dependencies, version, environment, stderr, timeout, output size, and symlink boundaries cannot be substituted by caller-controlled state."`
- Reviewer question: `"The suite mixes adversarial fake CLIs with a real pinned-package fixture; how far does its three-artifact graph assurance reach and what migration or provider behavior remains outside it?"`
- Answer: `"It proves package and lock provenance, sanitized noninteractive execution, fixed argv and cwd, bounded time and output, strict payload parsing, safe paths, and graph consistency. One fixture invokes the real pinned package against copied project schemas and a synthetic v2 change, including dependency readiness for the three artifacts. It does not execute the legacy migration orchestrator or a real PlanReview provider, so semantic evidence and end-to-end migration remain separate boundaries."`

- Protected invariant: `"A PlanReview remains bound to one exact investigation, execution and guard semantic target, generation, policy set, evidence namespace, and reviewer assignment. - Reviewer question: `"Does provider-schema validation alone enforce the scope rule and current three-artifact binding, and does this in-memory suite prove that a provider saw the same bytes?"`
- Answer: `"No. The tests show that the code-native validator rejects contradictory-only challenges and missing-scope no-challenge, and they exercise target components, immutable namespaces, and stale dependency identities using constructed objects. Separate provider-adapter coverage establishes prompt inclusion, while orchestrator and worker tests establish durable invocation and replacement behavior. The in-memory cases do not prove provider input consumption or live model quality."`

- Why: `"This is the commit-side integration consumer of investigation.json, execution.json, and plan-review.json. - Protected invariant: `"Plan commit remains one engine-owned commit over the exact validated planning tree with no foreign staging or free-form authority. - Reviewer question: `"The tests use real Git but fake OpenSpec and preconstructed v2 assurance; which transactional guarantees are proved and which investigation and provider guarantees are assumed?"`
- Answer: `"They prove path and trailer scope, clean-index preconditions, task-history rules, activation checks, stale-target refusal, change-lock exclusion, post-validation byte checks, branch and HEAD CAS, report binding, and rollback under filesystem and Git races. Most schema cases use a fake OpenSpec executable, and the v2 success fixture supplies prior assurance rather than running a live investigation or provider. The suite therefore consumes, but does not create, trustworthy three-artifact assurance."`

- Why: `"This suite is the executable provider boundary for Claude and Codex: fixed adapters, managed prompts, immutable request inputs, governed-projection observation, native output parsing, resource limits, and repository-wide launch slots. It is the direct consumer that must enforce the revised timeout contract without broadening unrelated controls."`
- Protected invariant: `"Only reviewed executable candidates and provider-specific auth context may launch; plan-review alone receives the scope-assessment instruction; all governed inputs are rebound before and after execution; native plus normalized output remains capped at 1048576 bytes; repository-wide concurrency remains capped at 2 unless a tracked policy deliberately lowers it; and a request timeout may be at most 600000 ms, with lower historical or fixture requests such as 300000 ms remaining valid and unchanged."`
- Reviewer question: `"Does increasing the allowed timeout to 600000 ms also permit more output, more concurrent providers, or mutation of an already-created 300000 ms request?"`
- Answer: `"No. Timeout, aggregate output, and concurrency are independent fields and tests. The runner rejects output over 1048576 bytes including Codex's semantic file and normalized output, rejects a third concurrent launch under the repository cap of 2, and honors lower limits. Request bytes are immutable and digest-bound, so 300000 ms fixture requests remain 300000 ms; 600000 is only the maximum accepted timeout."`

- Why: `"This contract suite defines provider identity, role independence, immutable invocation requests, structured process results, and hard resource maxima before any adapter or worker persists an outcome. It supplies the code-level source of truth for the timeout correction."`
- Protected invariant: ``"Provider requests are deeply immutable, assignment and digest bound, read-only, and accept only positive integer limits within independent maxima. `MAX_PROVIDER_LIMITS.timeoutMs` is exactly 600000, `aggregateOutputBytes` remains exactly 1048576, and concurrency remains governed separately at 2 by tracked adapter policy. Process failure, malformed or oversized output, binding mismatch, and observed mutation never become successful role results."``
- Reviewer question: `"Does the 600000 assertion mean every provider invocation must run for ten minutes or that output and concurrency were widened in the same change?"`
- Answer: `"No. It is an upper bound, not a required duration; lower per-request timeouts remain valid and are part of the immutable request digest. The output maximum is separately asserted and remains 1048576 bytes, while concurrency is not a request field and remains limited by the tracked policy at 2."`

- Why: `"This suite crosses the durable invocation and worker boundary for PlanReview. Its successful case pins the semantic output schema digest and persists a valid no-challenge scopeAssessment with bound evidence and no findings."`
- Protected invariant: `"The worker consumes the exact request-bound schema, evaluator, policy, and per-invocation limits, validates the returned semantic output, and stores the digest of the typed output. A no-challenge assessment must retain its evidence and cannot be accepted merely because the outer provider report succeeded. Lower requests such as 12345 or 300000 ms remain exactly bound; the repository maximum of 600000 ms does not widen the 1048576-byte output ceiling or concurrency-two policy."`
- Reviewer question: `"Does this successful no-challenge fixture prove the negative category matrix, that the provider received the explanatory prompt, or that its 300000 ms request should be rewritten to the new 600000 ms maximum?"`
- Answer: `"No. It proves a valid submission can pass the worker and be persisted under the expected PLAN_REVIEW_OUTPUT_SCHEMA digest. It uses a stub runner and supplies the semantic object directly. Its 12345 and 300000 ms values are deliberately lower immutable request limits, not stale declarations of the maximum; they remain valid without changing the 1 MiB aggregate-output or concurrency-two limits. Cross-field rejection is covered by plan-review.contract.test and instruction delivery plus runner bounds by provider-adapters.integration.test."`

- Protected invariant: `"Activation is inherited from reviewed Git ancestry and exact pinned contract bytes. Once active, a descendant cannot downgrade by deleting local metadata or the marker, and every v2 change must carry the complete validated investigation, execution, and plan-review artifact set."`
- Reviewer question: `"Is the literal monotonic declaration or artifact-name enum alone sufficient to stop a stale branch from selecting the legacy grammar or omitting an engine artifact?"`
- Answer: `"No. The engine resolves activation from the candidate parent and each applicable configured protected base, finds the reviewed anchor in ancestry, requires the exact activation marker, and then applies the v2 schema and managed validators. In this repository the first-touch anchor introduced the reviewed blob. The schema file states the contract; code must still reject downgrade, missing artifacts, and drift, and anchor discovery should not be overstated as hashing the historical anchor commit."`

## Risks / Trade-offs

- **[Risk] The new schema strands this active change or invalidates history** → Keep explicit legacy/v2 schema IDs, select by the governing plan parent's immutable marker, self-migrate T1.5 before activation completes, and test local plus historical CI replay.
- **[Risk] A plan selects legacy after cutover** → Derive activation from ancestry plus the configured protected baseline; require v2 after the first activation anchor even when a stale branch deletes or omits the marker.
- **[Risk] CI relies on unavailable local objects or transcripts** → Embed every non-reconstructable semantic fact in compact tracked artifacts; leave only raw events and mutable state in `.git`.
- **[Risk] A broad reviewer term creates a denial of service** → Use one fixed projector with code-capped per-source, per-term, total-hit, byte, and time budgets; retain rejected terms as audited proposals.
- **[Risk] A behavioral change claims an investigation exemption** → Use a closed category set, bind exact scope/baseline/intent and policy, require PlanReview to challenge eligibility, reject named high-risk classes, and never translate exemption into breadth/depth claims.
- **[Risk] Conservative grouping hides distinct semantics** → Start with small deterministic groups, require exact exceptions, fail ambiguous overlap, and let plan review split/promote groups.
- **[Risk] Plausible WHY is false** → Bind complete blobs and sharp questions, preserve actor authorship, run independent challenge and later tests, and never label field presence as proved understanding.
- **[Risk] Provider read-only flags are weaker than claimed** → Verify a broad governed projection before/after, use fixed read-only tool profiles, fail on observed drift, and retain explicit same-user/unobservable-write residuals.
- **[Risk] Executable discovery runs an attacker-controlled PATH entry** → Use only code-reviewed absolute candidates, canonicalize and probe them, record actual identity/version, and treat absence as provider unavailability.
- **[Risk] Background provider work corrupts mutable state after CLI exit** → Give each invocation one output directory and lease, use immutable results plus CAS refs, use short locks only, and recover/retry explicitly.
- **[Risk] Actor hints conflict and invert reviewer selection** → Collect all hints, pause on conflict, and never count disputed identity toward provider independence.
- **[Risk] A signed grant is mistaken for admissible review content** → Keep reservation separate from result admission, require an exact discriminated content form, consume only after pure validation, and replay aggregate use uniqueness in CI.
- **[Risk] The pulled-forward finalize shortcut is mistaken for crash-safe atomicity** → Name it and render CLI output as a projected single-pass substrate, promise exact rollback only for caught ordinary failures, keep persisted reports schema-compatible, keep `workflow commit` separate, retain the legacy sequence, and leave durable interruption recovery/commit transaction to T2.3.
- **[Risk] A candidate checkout silently consumes an external or stale dependency tree** → Do not relax runner-root containment or use a detached-worktree `node_modules` symlink; execute the narrow substrate against the current session-pinned repository/dependency installation and make any later isolated view prove its dependency source independently.
- **[Trade-off] Transition-specific grants can require two TTY actions on a degraded plan** → Prefer exact one-use binding in V1; healthy two-provider paths require zero grants, and later empirical data may justify a bounded session grant.
- **[Trade-off] Exact authored Markdown identity stales review on harmless edits** → Accept conservative replay cost rather than risk treating a negation or formatting-sensitive change as equivalent.
- **[Trade-off] Tracked hit/group evidence can grow** → Bound terms/hits, store raw transcripts only at runtime, and prefer compact selectors; correctness and remote replay take precedence over an opaque local-only report.

## Migration Plan

1. Plan-commit this legacy-schema T1.5 change with all tasks unchecked and no new workflow authority claim.
2. After Task 1.1 is committed with no active session, plan-commit this revision, implement the RED-first projected single-pass finalize substrate, and prove one check pass, exact checked/staged-tree equality, caught-failure restoration, no-staging-on-drift, separate commit, and legacy-path compatibility before using it on Task 2.1.
3. Implement RED-first evidence-node, canonicalization, provider/role, fake-adapter, and shared-validator contracts without enabling real launch or new planning gates.
4. Implement the binary-safe tracked-tree scanner, bounded term union, hit/group/disposition DAG, convergence/reuse validation, full-blob WHY records, and deterministic design projection behind non-default v2 contracts.
5. Evolve the strict adapter policy and diagnostic; add built-in Codex/Claude read-only adapters, durable invocation/pause/resume, actor conflict handling, and fake-backed mutation/failure tests. Run a non-authoritative local preflight; do not treat provider text as workflow authority.
6. Implement component canonicalization, planning generations, exact PlanReview, challenge/term projection, and the separate collaboration-grant lifecycle; then close the granted-result union, exemption branch, main-term metadata, PlanReview severity/risk/uncertainty, and shared use validators before live readiness consumes any of them.
7. Integrate the same pure validators into live planning, task/report evidence, CI, and archive; implement explicit introduction/revision selection, historical legacy/v2 replay, the legacy migration transition, then the activation anchor and v2 default in that order. Prove that the activation task can finish under its already-pinned legacy session and that replay selects the correct generation on both sides of the anchor.
8. With no task session active, run the governed legacy-migration form of `workflow propose` for T1.5, fill the engine-produced main/WHY envelopes, obtain PlanReview (or an exact human-granted degraded result if an alternate is unavailable), switch the change to `expense-app-v2`, and create a normal planning revision. Preserve completed task projections and verify historical legacy replay.
9. Update executable guidance and Roadmap ownership notes, run all registered non-database checks, perform an independent exact-diff review of the trust core, run healthy and explicitly authorized degraded pilots as applicable, and archive through the existing transition after merge.

Rollback before v2 activation is an ordinary managed revert while legacy remains the default. After the marker/default and migrated plan merge, rollback must preserve historical schema selection and either keep v2 support or introduce a new explicit successor migration; deleting the marker or rewriting archived evidence is not a rollback mechanism. Collaboration grant state is revoked/terminally cleaned without deleting signed evidence.

## Open Questions

None. Artifact names, schema/cutover discriminator, tracked/runtime boundary, term and provider budgets, grouping baseline, actor conflicts, provider resolution, PlanReview canonicalization/currentness, grant scope and no-adapter degradation, wrapper checkpoints, the projected single-pass/ordinary-failure boundary, and remaining T1.5/T2 ownership are fixed by this design.
